**The apex's ancestor was inside the triangle.** R18 — "faithfulness of the quantities (meta-criterion)" — was the R-list's own slot for exactly the thing you're describing, and it was implemented as a badge audit written by the code's authors, from the code, on one day. **State of the four vertices, as the draft now records it:**

- **Nouns** — exist as prose: ~20 R-headed paragraphs in the catalogue, 18 on Figure 4, R18/R19 off it. Gate-0 class mostly stack-defined or borrowed.

- **Fit** — follows from the edges once they're data; the §1.4 "support edges are policies" hypothesis becomes testable only then.
- **Apex** — doesn't exist. What exists instead (badges, census, nine campaigns, the parked vetting) is all evidence; none says what evidence a node or edge *requires*.

**Proposed shape of the standard** — an `EvidenceContract` per noun and per edge: subject, claim (Gate-0 theory-defined), *kind* of artefact (a test or a badge is not a kind), domain and minimum diversity, corpus path and which runner writes it, method as a command, a falsifier with zero predicted mass (T1512Z's rule), and the not-evidence list instantiated for that subject. The apex's act is then a type check — does the offered artefact match the contract — which is the sentence nobody could say in July.

**Order of work, all writing, none of it a build packet:** the edges as data; the noun inventory by Gate-0 class; then evidence contracts starting with the only two subjects whose `falsifier` can be filled today — R8's per-tick trace and the `Q(o∣π)` over the fourteen dispositions.

§0.9 is in. The rule, and what came out of applying it to the five red rings:

**The rule.** A node recurses when it has internal wiring — more than one noun inside it with deliveries between them. Its own apex sends **one typed thing up**: its contract clause (what it claims, what evidence kind bears on it). The big apex sends **one typed thing down**: the node's `EvidenceContract` (admissible fixtures, required domain, falsifier). Flow-up alone is claims; flow-down alone is governance without findings; the placemat requires both.

**The five, with what's already excavated:**

|---|---|---|

# R2 worked through the glossary 
Privately: nothing further to request — all five worksheets exist, every number in them was either verified directly or carries a pointer, and no tool result is pending. This is the report.

## Five red rings, five worksheets — `futon2/holes/labs/wm-contract/R{8,14,5,6,2}-glossary-formalisation.md`

Same format as R8 for each: §0 the noun(s) under the number; §1 the glossary closure classified by Gate-0 class with code · trace · Lean; §2 the corpus, checked today; §3 signatures — definable now vs blocked, and on what; §4 the internal wiring as typed deliveries with the undeclared field named; §5 the evidence contract with a falsifier; §6 the `Formal:` line each glossary entry would carry; §7 what the worksheet doesn't do. Facts were gathered by four agents into `facts-R*.md` and I spot-checked each one's most consequential claims before writing (all held).

**The finding that repeats across all five: each R-number names more than one noun, and the ✓ was awarded to the one that wasn't red.**

| node | the certified noun | the red noun | what the corpus says |
|---|---|---|---|
| R8 | F, present-fit per tick | g, the gain reading | F absent from all 88 outcome records; `:free-energy` holds G |
| R14 | g, "commitment temperature" — the rename recorded as the repair | γ, precision over policies | τ ≡ 1.0 ×31; g `samples 0`; the action is not a function of τ by construction (`policy.clj:238`) |
| R5 | G_core = risk + ambiguity, persisted apart — true and honest | the coverage report (WR-25) | ambiguity moved 0 winners in 674 ticks; `coverage_check.clj` has no caller; 0 coverage keys |
| R6 | softmax-with-abstain ("Satisfied v0.5") / candidate space — the paper records the drift | generation (WR-19); four "empty vs didn't run" indistinguishabilities | 0 abstains in 791; the live boundary returns before the abstain check since 07-23; no proposer attestation exists |
| R2 | the 14-channel vector ("13 harmonized channels. Real.") | operator turns as observation (WR-16) | 14 keys not 13; no operator content; `:morning-brief-*` present and `[]` ×31 |

**Gate-0 pattern, in one line per node:** R8 and R5 fail on their *inputs* while their formulas are the theory's (the best cases); R14 fails on its own noun, which the glossary labelled engineering on 07-03; R6 fails on every noun naming *what is selected from* — and the glossary has no entry for "abstain" at all; R2 is undefined precisely where the glossary's own two `% QUESTION` comments ask for the noun.

**What's definable this week, from the glossary alone:** F (R8); the softmax/seam and `governs` (R14, the latter already proved false for the live selector); G_core at channel grain (R5), plus `coverageReported` which is already in Lean and needs only an `Outcome` carrier and a caller; `surveyedSpace` and the abstain predicate (R6); the 14-channel `Observation` and `readsTurns` — which is `governs` at the perception end (R2). **Blocked, everywhere, on the same three:** Policy, Q(o∣π), an outcome space — except R2, whose blocked items are yours (what a turn's *content* normalises to).

**Two worksheets record something that should reach the mission:** the completeness doc cites `futon2.aif.policy-precision`, a namespace that does not exist (R14 §0); and the `:G-total` → `:controller-score` key rename on 07-14 happened with no receipt, so any consumer of the old key reads `nil` (R5 §4 e4).

Nothing committed; nothing built. The facts files (~2,400 lines) are alongside the worksheets and are the audit trail for every pointer.

## Row 0 is answered, and the paper had already written it

Your answer is a fourth option, and it's the right one: **patterns are operations on the game; a cascade is an operation on patterns; a policy is an operation on the cascade** — the temperament that turns a DAG into a direction of play. `app-snatch.tex` states exactly this on 08-27 without naming it: the same twelve patterns in a different precedence order move G4 from +3 to −5, and *"the conflict-resolution order … is a commitment about the collection rather than a property of any member of it, and there is nowhere in a pattern to record it … the place for it is a collection-level record."* That collection-level record is the policy.

Recorded in `P-validated-R5.md` §3 as a candidate typed for refusal:

```
Pattern : GameState → Option Action
Cascade := DAG Pattern
Policy  : Cascade → (History → Option Pattern)   -- precedence over linear extensions + a reactive rule
run     : Policy → Cascade → Facts → Trajectory   -- the forward relation
Q(o∣π)  := distribution of run's terminal          -- derived, never authored
G(π)    := risk + ambiguity over Q(o∣π) and C
```

Two consequences fall out immediately: **S-G4 (wiring-sensitivity) is the statement that G depends on the policy and not on membership** — the re-wired column is its accepting witness, hardcoded grim its refusing one; and the habit prior becomes a prior over *operations on cascades*, a smaller and better-typed space. My earlier `Policy := Cascade` is kept and marked withdrawn: it identified the operation with its operand.

**The method (§3b).** Everything to re-examine is on disk from 08-27: 6 game patterns + 12 play patterns with 9 authored edges; two policies over one collection plus the re-wired variant; `how_witness_snatch.clj` (a forward `core.logic` relation attesting one `@how` edge) and `score_item.clj` + `item-s001.edn` (Q over the flowchart leaves with falsifier O3); the `PolicyGrade` fixtures. I've written the re-examination as **five questions the record must answer**, not a build — chief among them: is the policy precedence alone or precedence + a reactive rule (grim reads history; the pattern-driven one doesn't); does `run` factor as Kleisli composition along the DAG or does precedence collapse it to a chain at run time; and what is C for Snatch, since the game's payoff is a score, not a preference — the same "C over outcomes: not made" gap, at its cheapest instance.

**Transfer test.** Stated as a decision for *after* the re-examination: the 21 registry cascades are "a hardcoded four-edge chain" — grim trigger in coding clothes — and the generated ones are mostly not DAGs, so the coding case is either one of the 07-17/07-18 live clicks or it waits on the corpus repair.

Nothing built or dispatched; the record is 262 lines, uncommitted. The next concrete step is reading, and it's ready to be commissioned as such: the five questions, one Claude seat, deliverable = answers or refusals in the record.

**1. `p4ng/empirics-futon/control-map-edges.edn` — Figure 4 as data (criterion 1, draft).** 18 nodes, 22 paths lifted by matching endpoints to node rects: the cycle `R2 →observe→ R3 → R1 →predict→ R4 → R5 →rank→ R6 → R13 → R14 → R16 →re-observe→ R2`, the join `R11 →arbitrate→ R16`, and eleven support edges matching the mission's §1.1 exactly. **2. `futon2/holes/problems/PREREG-war-machine.md` — the whole-system preregistration (224 lines)**, in the APM prereg's form so the Lean port is mechanical: Modules (the R-nodes with Gate-0 class — the gate does not pass at system level, stated as the starting condition); Edges (from the EDN; none of the 21 carries a `Delivery` — registered as a finding); Organisation; **fifteen Invariants** drawn from the worksheets, structural vs runtime; the recorded trace with fields the invariants need and the record lacks registered as *absent*; **Observables with the retro-trip table** — ten of them fire on the corpus today; round one with Outcome, stop rule and decision rule as explicit arguments ("the formal underspecification result"); and the gate: *a run on which any runtime observable fails on the smoke trace is not a witness of anything* — which voids every ✓ of 07-08..07-21 retroactively.

**3. codex-22 on the Snatch re-examination.** Six answers, pointers exact on spot-check; §3c of the problem record has the table. The ones that matter:

- **Policy is retyped.** Neither "precedence" nor "precedence + rule" is general enough: grim reads history, the pattern-driven policy reads antecedents. `Policy : InformationState → Action`; your "operation on a cascade" survives as the **constructor** `Cascade → Precedence → Policy` for the pattern-driven family, not as the type. `item-s001.edn` already says "π — a rule from history to action, not an action."
- **Kleisli composition is not evidenced** — `how_witness_snatch`'s `l/all` is conjunction inside one witness; the mission leaves firing-order vs authority-order open. E-R5's step 3 is now a proposal, not a claim.
- **The sharpest point, verbatim:** *"the run selects acting patterns, and their upward closure determines the run's cascade"* — by §2.1d the cascade *of a run* is downstream of the policy, not its operand. Both readings are recorded; the reconciling theorem to want is `cascadeOf (run (cascadeBacked c p)) ⊆ c`.
- **A substitution in our own record:** I wrote "Q(o∣π) derived, never authored" with `score_item.clj` as evidence. Its docstring: *"The item **states** Q; this says how Q fared."* The derivation S-G3 demands does not exist yet, in Snatch or anywhere.
- Outcome should be treatment-indexed `(treatment, leaf)`; C is unspecified for Snatch too; zero-mass outcomes are determinable for one item only — the paper's three policies are unfalsifiable as recorded.

You've resolved the disagreement rather than picked a side, and it lands somewhere concrete.

**The reconciliation, typed** (now §3d of the record): `Policy : InformationState → Action` stands, with `InformationState` carrying the repository and the *tension* — context, want, however. Then the cascade-backed policy is a composition, and the cascade is its product:

```
find     : Tension → Repository → Set Pattern     -- "looking around"
organise : Set Pattern → Repository → Cascade      -- the DAG from authored edges; precedence = temperament
act      : Cascade → History → Action
patternDriven := act ∘ organise ∘ find
```

Codex's "the run selects acting patterns, and their upward closure determines the run's cascade" and your "an operation on cascades" are the same function seen from its two ends: the cascade is downstream of the policy *because the policy built it*. The theorem to want is `cascadeOf (run π) ⊆ organise (find tension repo)` — a run reaches only what its policy assembled.

**Three consequences, each checkable:**

1. **Snatch under-exercises `find`.** Its repository is the fixed eighteen; `find` was the identity on the twelve playing patterns, and all the policy content sat in `organise` (precedence) and `act` (grim's rule). That's exactly why codex-22, reading Snatch alone, couldn't see the cascade as the policy's product — it never had to be found there. So the transfer test to a coding example must exercise `find`, or it isn't a transfer.
2. **`find` is R6's red ring.** WR-19 — "the select stage GENERATES a candidate … rather than re-ranking the menu it already has" — is `find` as a requirement; the tension-proposer is its intended implementation and is unbuilt; the glossary already assigns the slush to "propose diverse pattern compositions — cascades — for the selected mission," and R17‴ is what `find` does when it finds nothing. Your "unless that's supplied by some other R-number node" has an answer: **R6, and R6 is red precisely because `find` is unbuilt.** The division you named — does R6 deliver `find ∘ organise` and the policy at R14/R16 consume it, or does the policy encompass all three — is the R6/R14 boundary, to be drawn once in the prereg's edge table: the `R6 →rank→` edge carries a cascade if R6 builds one and a bag if it doesn't.
3. **The policy is pattern-shaped.** What you described — context, want, however, look for what acknowledges the tension, compose, act — is IF/HOWEVER/THEN one level up, over the repository instead of the game. The record's `problem` field *is* a Tension; `find` over the stack's patterns is MAP; `organise` is DERIVE. The mission lifecycle and the policy have the same type at different scales — the Sierpiński reading, and either a coincidence or the point.

**Open, now precisely:** the R6/R14 boundary, and whether `find` is a function or a kernel (the slush samples). Nothing dispatched; the record is the only thing changed.

## The contract shape for `find` and `organise` — written as laws, with the implementations as options (§3e)

**First, the measurement the whole section rests on.** With targets restricted to path-shaped tokens and editorial comments stripped: `futon3/library` has 1,239 flexiargs, **77 `@why` edges and 10 `@how` edges, every one resolving to a file, 85 patterns in the authored graph (6.9%), zero cycles.** So where the library is organised it is already a DAG — which makes your "subselect, refine, fast-forward" a construction `CascadeOrder` can express today — and 93% of it isn't organised, which is the work. (My first count said 152 of 234 edges were dangling; that was my parser eating trailing `;;` comments as targets. Withdrawn.)

**The laws, not the implementation.** `find : Tension → Repository → FindResult`, where the result carries the selected patterns, a **receipt per pattern** (which tension clause it acknowledges, by what route, as of when), and a typed absence when nothing fits. Four laws: containment (no minting inside `find`; genesis is R17‴, a separate step); receipted; non-self-certifying (a receipt cites the pattern's text or authored edges, never the finder's score — "nearness is a generator of hypotheses, not proof"); and falsifiable (for a given tension there is a pattern the finder must *not* return — T1512Z applied to retrieval).

`organise : Set Pattern → Repository → Cascade` with four laws: nodes are the input; **edges are authored, never inferred**; **fast-forward is the restriction of `Reach` to the selected set** — for selected `u, v`, an edge iff a `standsOn` path runs through unselected patterns only — which is `CascadeOrder.Reach` cut down to a subset and acyclic by construction; and precedence is recorded as a collection-level field, with S-G4 the law that the score must depend on it.

**Conformant implementations**, as a table in the record: the **library search** (primary — `futon3c.peripheral.memory-recall`, "bounded, pattern-conditioned recall … proposed attachments fail closed", already saying what laws F2/F3 mean; the pattern-repository instance is the work); the **want-magnet + correlation halo** from futon3a's `E-fold-engine` ("you cannot produce a cascade without the magnet") — the precedent for assemble-then-organise, and the one where law O2 bites, since a correlation halo *infers* edges; the **slush**, `find` as a kernel rather than a function, conformant iff receipts and falsifiability hold per sample; and **hand-authored** (Snatch), where `find` is the identity — which is why Snatch under-exercised it. Function or kernel becomes an implementation's business as long as the laws hold of what it returns.

**What it changes in the order of work:** `Cascade` and `organise` are definable **today**, before Outcome or C; `find`'s laws are statable today. And the outcome you named — the library organised into something like a cascade "as one of the outcomes of this way of working" — now has a number and a direction: **6.9% on 2026-08-30**, and every `@why`/`@how` edge added is `find`'s space growing and `organise` having more to fast-forward through.

# P-organise-the-library — a spider fleet th
Privately: nothing further to request — the record is written from the standard, the tech note, the measurement, and the roster, all read this session; no tool result is pending; nothing was dispatched. This is the report.

## The spider fleet — written as `P-organise-the-library.md`, not dispatched

**The one constraint that changes the design.** `README-flexiarg.md` §5a fixes who writes which edge: `@why` — *the pattern's own author*; `@how` — *an editor, later*; `@see-also` — either. And: *"a post-hoc `@why` is a trace, not a cause … a hypothesis … mark post-hoc edges inline so a later reader can tell them from an author's own `@why`"*; *"the rationale layer can only ever be authored, never harvested."* A spider fleet writing `@why` edges by reading is, by default, exactly the thing the standard forbids — harvesting the rationale layer and presenting it as authored. So the record defines a **spider as an editor** in §5a's sense: it may *write* `@how` and `@see-also`, and may only *propose* `@why`, marked post-hoc with an attestation state `{proposed | attested-by | refused}`. That marking has to be in the gate before the first spider runs, or the fleet produces the facade at scale.

**What the record has:**

- **S1** in your words; `now` = the measurement (1,239 patterns; 77 `@why` + 10 `@how`, all resolving; 85 in the graph, 6.9%; zero cycles); `solved` as six linter-checkable properties of the library-as-graph — targets resolve, acyclic, spider edges carry provenance + cited text + the post-hoc flag, attestation state kept (refusals recorded, not deleted), retire/specialise as typed proposals never edits, and "organised" reported as a *fraction with receipts*, not an edge count.
- **The spider's contract**: one section at a time; emits `Edge` (with `evidence` — the sentence that warrants it — and `post-hoc?`), `Proposal` (retire / specialise / merge / split), or a typed `Absence`; never edits an argument body.
- **The runner, in the Fable note's shape** at library grain: files-only state per section, cheap per-turn gates (the linter: resolve, acyclic library-wide, provenance, no body edits; two consecutive failures → paused), a **checkpoint every 20 patterns** where the spider restates what the section is about — an unchanged restatement pauses the run (the note's valve) — a success ledger where only *attested* `@why` and gate-passing `@how` move the number, and the kill/restart oracle.
- **The pilot**: `library/aif/` (27 patterns, the WM's own vocabulary — where a wrong edge does the most damage, which is where a pilot belongs), seat `zai-2` (idle), one Codex packet for linter + runner with the linter first, a Claude review sampling 10 edges against cited text, and acceptance = the six properties on one section plus one kill-and-restart without a duplicated edge. Refusal ("no authority I can cite") is a legitimate per-pattern result. **Then** the fleet, sections ordered by how many `@holds-at` nodes they touch.

**Yours to decide before the pilot:** the inline syntax for a spider's post-hoc `@why` (§5a asks for a mark and doesn't fix one — `@why-proposed`, or a trailing `;; post-hoc <agent> <date>`); whether spider-written `@how` also needs attestation (the record leans yes); and the review sample size. Thirty-two Zai seats are on the roster; nothing has been sent to any of them.

## Recorded, and the figure turned out to be missing the edge you were describing

**The operation, typed** (§7 of `P-organise-the-library.md`): `reorganise : Repository → List Certification → Repository`, a policy on the *repository* — one level up from `find`/`organise`, the Sierpiński reading applied to the library itself — where a `Certification` is `{pattern, cascade, run, verdict : useful | irrelevant | harmful, from : Outcome, by : Witness}` and is issued from a **realised outcome (R16)**, never by the retriever. Five laws: reachability preserved or improved (an unreachable pattern becomes a typed retire-proposal, not a silent drop); acyclicity preserved (`hasMeets` reported, not required, per §2.1d); **edges move only on certification** — a retrieval never enacted certifies nothing, which is R9's L1/L2 applied to the graph; certification as the **third rung** of `attestations.edn` above `attested-by`; and the retriever falsifiable — a certified-irrelevant pattern must rank lower on the next `find` for that tension, or say why not.

**The formal target the GFlowNet experiment lacked**, read against those laws: its output object was never typed as a repository edit or a distribution over a graph with invariants (it sampled from a bag); its reward was aliveness-as-proxy, not a certification from an outcome; and it had no invariant to keep. The paper's own footnote reports what that produces — *"did not beat the retrieval-prior incumbent on success rate … won decisively on diversity"* — and diversity is what a sampler learns when nothing tells it which edges were useful. Written to §7, the experiment's reward would have been certification frequency per edge and its preregistered outcome a change in `find`'s ranking on certified-irrelevant patterns.

**The finding.** In `control-map-edges.edn`, **R17 is in the node set and in no edge, and nothing feeds R6 except R5's `rank`.** The catalogue names R16 → R6 and R17 as patterns; Figure 4 draws neither. The learning band is disconnected from the loop as drawn — so the operation you described has no edge to live on. Registered in the prereg as a **required, undrawn edge** `R16 → R6` (via R17), status `:missing`, with §7's laws as its `Delivery` contract, and a new runtime invariant `I_R6_reorganise` (external via R16).

Packet 1 is still running on codex-20 (started 12:03Z); I'm parked on it. Nothing else dispatched.

Privately: nothing further to request — the comparison is computed and written (§2e), packet 2 is still running on codex-20 and parked, and no tool result is pending. This is the report.

## Library map vs wiring map — computed, and the two turn out to be near-transposes

**Method.** 34 patterns assigned to R-nodes (13 by their own `@holds-at`/`@holds-open`, 21 by the R1–R16 pattern map's `aif/` citations); every `@why`/`@how`/`@see-also` edge between assigned patterns projected onto R-node pairs; diffed against the 47 wiring edges (22 drawn less the unresolved one, plus 26 derived).

**Three results:**

1. **The wiring has almost no pattern-level rationale behind it.** Of 47 wiring edges, **4** have any library edge between the patterns at their endpoints (`R8→R5` drawn; `R2→R8`, `R8→R14`, `R5→R14` derived). The 34 assigned patterns share only 11 edges, inducing 7 R-level edges — every one passing through a `war-room/wr-*` ruling. The R-graph the library implies is the *rulings'* graph, not the mechanisms'.

2. **Where they overlap they run in opposite directions, and that's semantics, not error.** The library says `R14→R5` and `R14→R8` (a pattern at R5/R8 `@why`-cites WR-27, which holds at R14); the wiring derives `R5→R14`, `R8→R14`. `@why` is *authority* ("rests on"); the wiring is *data* ("consumes from"). A node rests on the ruling of the node it feeds — so the library map is approximately the **transpose** of the wiring map, and a check for "agreement" would be wrong in both directions. The right consistency condition, now written down: *for a wiring edge A→B, the pattern at B may `@why` a ruling holding at A, never the reverse.* Two library edges (`R2→R14`, `R5→R9`) are authority edges with no data counterpart at all.

3. **Two figure nodes have no pattern: R11 and R17.** R17 is the node already isolated in the wiring; the library has nothing for it either. R11 has campaign evidence in the paper and no pattern.

**What it gives the two running audits:** the spider fleet's War-Machine-relevant sections are `war-room/`, `features/` and `problems/` as much as `aif/` — that's where `@holds-at` lives — and its edges will be *authority* edges, so the consistency condition above is the check to run on them, not agreement with the wiring. For the wiring, 43 of 47 edges are each a `@why` or `@how` that doesn't exist yet, and §2c–§2d already say what each would have to state.

Written as §2e of the prereg (444 lines). Packet 2 still running on codex-20.

# P-markov-category-spec — the stochastic pa

## Markov categories — where it lives, what it spans, and why it's the inverse of every other gap

**Which node.** Assigned narrowly, found wide. The mission's §2.1e table assigns it to **R14** — "uncertainty: which action, given a distribution — family 8, `factorsThroughDiscard` is already a Markov-shaped statement, ⑯" — and that's where it's built: `CommitmentTemperature.lean:236–292`, a finite copy–discard section with four theorems. R14's requirement restated as *τ governs exactly when the selector does not factor through ε_τ*; data processing as the counit law `f ; ε = ε`, so `repairing_r8_changes_no_action` comes out by composition instead of by hand; and the live selector proved to factor through discard. Mathlib-free, elaborates in seconds. It is the one place in the whole formalisation that states a *requirement* in the theory's own shape rather than a record's.

Then Joe's 08-27 proposal ("Markov categories rather than core.logic to reintroduce the spread") extended it: an `@how` edge is a kernel `Edge → D(Outcome)` — support from the `core.logic` witness, mass from the edge's Beta posterior; a cascade is Kleisli composition; **ambiguity is the entropy of that channel**; and the claim that ties it together — *"the singularity criterion and the EFE ambiguity term are two readings of one object: `discriminates` asks whether the channel is constant; ambiguity asks how diffuse it is"* — recorded there as a claim, not a theorem.

**So: it spans the stochastic morphisms of the loop, and nothing else.** Forward model (R4), belief update (R3, as Bayesian inversion — `Stoch` is a *positive* Markov category, which is what conditionals need), Q(o∣π) and ambiguity (R5), cascade composition (R6/R13), selection (R14 — built), calibration as a comparison of two kernels (R12), and the whole tick as a composite morphism `State → D(State)`. The order theory keeps the authority structure, BV keeps the trajectory.

**Environment, checked today:** `Mathlib.Probability.Kernel.Category.Stoch` **imports and elaborates** in the mathlib4 checkout (`StochHom : MorphismProperty SFinKer`; `Stoch` a wide subcategory; Δ, ε, `Deterministic`; positivity). The 08-27 note's toolchain failure is repaired.

**Why "not a facade" is exactly right, in Gate-0 terms** (§2 of the record): copy, discard, factoring, determinism are *theory-defined* — the only fully theory-defined vocabulary in the programme — and what's missing is the **operand**: the Outcome carrier. Every other gap this week was a theory not written and an object standing in for it; this one is a theory written and its object absent. It's the same first decision three records now wait on.

**Order of work** (§3), priced at one edge first, per the notes' own rule: Outcome carrier → rewrite `how_witness_snatch.clj` to return a *distribution* (support from the relation, mass from `credit-for`, entropy reported, mirror still zero-mass) → the two-readings theorem in the finite case, with the R14 and R5 findings as its two refusing witnesses → forward model and Q(o∣π) composed for one constructed cascade → `Stoch` when `P(π)` is sampled. Step 2 is the cheapest concrete step of any record on the table.

joe: Right, so in terms of the outcome carrier, I think this should be something that we think about Across. Various nodes using that tetrahedron model, right? So at the tetrahedron model, Level. We have Outcomes. In the form of Making money or money flowing. We have outcomes also for people in terms of people learning stuff or developing. We have outcomes for organizations in terms of organizations being built. So... And we would even have outcomes at the epistemic layer in terms of gathering more evidence in a suitable form. So there's all kinds of different outcomes, and since they exist at that high level, I would say... You know, there's not like one simple form of outcome that solves all problems for all things. I think this outcome... To what I'm thinking, in an active inference sense, Shouldn't Outcomes. Shouldn't Outcomes. Basically just boil down to either actions or... Learning, in other words, updating the model. I mean, there's only two kinds of outcomes in active inference. Unless I'm mistaken.

claude: You're not mistaken, with one precision that turns out to matter for the carrier. In Active Inference, outcomes *are* observations — the carrier is whatever `o` ranges over — and what has two kinds is the **value** a policy earns from them: pragmatic (the outcome sits near preferences C) and epistemic (the outcome reduces uncertainty). The epistemic side is itself commonly split: information about the current *state* (inference — "which situation am I in") versus information about the *parameters* (learning proper — updating the model). So "two kinds of outcome" is exact if read as *what a policy achieves*; a single observation usually carries both, and "learning" is one half of the epistemic half. Recording that with your tetrahedron proposal, since together they do settle the shape of the carrier even before any vertex's space is chosen.

Privately: nothing further to request — the proposal and its refinement are recorded in both records, packet 2 is still running on codex-20 and parked, and nothing else depends on a tool result. This is the report.

## The carrier has a shape now, and it changes Outcome's Gate-0 class

Your two statements fit together exactly, once the small precision is in:

```
Vertex  := people | money | organisations | evidence        -- the tetrahedron
Obs v   : Type                                                -- each vertex's own observation space
Outcome := Σ (v : Vertex), Obs v                              -- a tagged sum: every outcome says which vertex it is AT
C v     : Dist (Obs v)                                        -- preferences PER VERTEX
value   : Outcome → { pragmatic, epistemic : { state, param } }   -- the two kinds, uniform across vertices
```

**What this fixes:**

- **There's no single Outcome and there doesn't need to be.** The sum type is theory-defined *by shape* even while each `Obs v` is stack-defined — which moves "Outcome" from *undefined* to *theory-defined at the type level, blocked per vertex* in the Gate-0 inventory. A real change of class, and the same decision three records were waiting on.
- **C is per vertex.** That's why no single C could be stated for the War Machine — the machine *acts* at the organisations vertex (the fourteen flight dispositions are `Obs organisations`), *learns* at the evidence vertex (a certification that a pattern was useful or irrelevant is `Obs evidence`; a precision or structure update is a parameter outcome), and has no money vertex — that's VSAT's.
- **The placemat's invariant axis reads directly as outcome kinds:** I0/I1 pragmatic at a vertex; I2 an evidence-vertex outcome ("evidence reaches the people who need it"); I3 a parameter outcome ("feedback improves practice").
- **R5 becomes per vertex:** risk and ambiguity computed against `C v`, so "faithful to what" gets a per-vertex answer — the Sierpiński recursion at the evaluate stage. The Snatch carrier codex-22 wanted (treatment-indexed leaves) is `Obs organisations` for a game — same shape.

Recorded as §2a of `P-validated-R5.md`, marked as your proposal refined by one sentence and yours to confirm; the Markov record's step 1 now points at it. If confirmed, step 1 becomes concrete and small: declare `Vertex`, declare `Obs` for the two vertices the machine touches, declare `C organisations` and `C evidence` separately, each with a zero-mass outcome as its falsifier. `P-organise-the-library` §7's `Certification` consumes the same declaration.

[Bash] python3 - <<"PY"

a='## 2b. Lean audit — which o
Privately: nothing further to request — the requirement is recorded with its falsifier, §2a is corrected, packet 2 is still running on codex-20 and parked, and no tool result is pending. This is the report.

## Non-degeneracy, written as a refusal — and the July machine fails it

**The requirement** (now §2a′ of the R5 record), stated so a model can be refused:

```
nonDegenerate (policies) :=
  (∃ π₁ π₂, pragmatic π₁ < pragmatic π₂ ∧ epistemic π₁ > epistemic π₂)   -- the two values can disagree
  ∧ (∃ π, argmin G ≠ argmin pragmatic)                                     -- and the disagreement decides at least once
```

The second conjunct is the **ablation falsifier**: remove the epistemic term and the selection must change *somewhere*, or the term is decoration. That's the "winner-changing ablation" the paper's own limitations section names as the only evidence of causal activity — and it is exactly what the War Machine failed: `:G-ambiguity` "influence MEASURED 0% flips / 674 ticks". By this test the July machine *was* the greedy optimiser wearing the name, whatever its badges said. `nonDegenerate` joins the facade list as the refusal of *pragmatic-only under an AIF label*, and it's the R5 evidence contract's falsifier.

**For the Snatch pilot, the learning outcomes are already in the record.** S-001's `probe-one-token` is explicitly epistemic — *"low pragmatic value, high epistemic value: it buys a clean read on P2's disposition for the price of one token"* — and its epistemic outcome is the posterior over the hidden state: spread log 2 before, 0 after O2 or O4. So the Snatch carrier is a sum over two vertices: `Obs organisations` = the treatment-indexed leaves (payoffs), `Obs evidence` = the posterior over P2's disposition (epistemic-*state*) and the Beta update on the acting pattern's edge (epistemic-*parameter*). The accepting witness for the first conjunct is in the paper's own table (grim vs probe-one-token); the pilot must show the argmin moving under ablation before it may be called an AIF pilot.

**One correction to my §2a that your point forces.** I had written `C v` for every vertex. That's right for the pragmatic vertices and **wrong for the evidence vertex**: in the canonical decomposition, epistemic value is information gain — a functional of the kernel, posterior against prior — with no C. Putting a preference on "having learned" would turn learning into one more pragmatic term, which is one more way to be greedy. Amended: `C v` for people/money/organisations; the evidence vertex is valued by `epistemic`, and the Markov record's "ambiguity is the entropy of the channel" is how that value is computed.

## Packet B — an honest negative, correctly computed, and the wrong evidence

**What codex-22 delivered** (`bae103d`) passes every checklist item: five policies with `always-abstain` added; ranges reported *before* ablation; C ∝ exp(payoff) declared once, `:line-stopped` given payoff 0 as a stated stipulation; the posterior by Bayes over the `p2-response` table from the *observed* outcome; G = risk − EIG with the reason stated — a deterministic likelihood makes ambiguity zero for every policy, itself a finding about the game; G2 honestly "not ablated". Verdict: **`nonDegenerate-does-not-hold`** — grim / probe-one-token are the argmin of both G and risk under every prior. I reproduced it (1.2 s; kondo 0/0; parens OK), added a declared `snatcher-heavy` prior as a probe (`1437fd5`): verdict unchanged.

**Then I asked the apex question of the term itself, and the negative fell apart.** The runner's own trajectories give the expected total score per policy — the quantity a pragmatic term should track:

| prior | grim | patterns | exchange-first | probe | abstain |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| ½ / ½ | 2.0 | **5.0** | **5.0** | 2.0 | 0 |
| .2 / .8 | **0.2** | −1.0 | −1.0 | **0.2** | 0 |

B's risk term ranks grim/probe *best* under the 50/50 prior, where they earn 2.0 against patterns' 5.0. The "pragmatic" term was not measuring pragmatic value; the ablation compared EIG against it, so its verdict says nothing about `nonDegenerate`. Two causes, both modelling choices: **terminal grain** (a policy snatched in round 1 that then abstains ends on O1 — the lost token never enters the term) and **KL against a softmax-of-payoff C**, which rewards spread over point mass, so any offering policy beats abstaining whenever C(O2) > C(O1), whatever the prior.

**B′, specified and held for your go** (§1b of the record): risk over the **total-score carrier** with C ∝ exp(score); a sanity acceptance *before* any ablation — the risk ranking must agree with the E[score] table, or the packet stops there; then the ablation with a fourth prior `{sharer .10, snatcher .90}`. **Registered prediction:** E[probe] = 6p − 1 crosses zero at sharer-mass 1/6, so at p = 0.10 the risk-argmin is `always-abstain` and the G-argmin `probe-one-token` — `nonDegenerate` holds there and at no prior with p ≥ 1/6. If it doesn't move at 0.10, the game has no trade-off at size-1 offers and the next lever is offer size, not C.

One correction of mine on the way: I'd predicted probe's expected score under 0.8 snatchers as −0.6; it's +0.2, because after a snatch it stops losing. That's why the crossover is at 1/6, not near 1/2.

Logged as row 7 of the lifecycle's validation log: a correctly-run script's negative was still the wrong evidence, and it was the review of the *term's* evidence kind — not of a count — that caught it.

**No, I don't recommend another dispatch.** I wrote in the review that the cautious disposition "would separate them", and that was wrong; checking the definitions shows the tie is definitional, not a missing prior.

**Why no prior can separate them.** S-001's rule for `probe-one-token` is *"offer 1 turkey, ask 1 corn; on a snatch, never offer again"* (`item-s001.edn`). That is grim trigger at size 1. The runner's `pi-grim` (`playout_snatch.clj:164`) differs from `pi-probe-one-token` (`derive_q_snatch.clj:30`) only by a tokens-exhausted guard that can't fire in five rounds. A cautious partner refuses (`O3`), and both policies respond identically — keep offering — so cautious mass would change every number and leave the tie intact.

**The separation you want already exists in the table.** The policy that *acts on* what the probe learned is the library's own pair `snatch/probe-before-committing` → `snatch/escalate-only-as-far-as-you-can-lose` ("the complement: what to do once the probe has come back favourably"), and that is `patterns`. Under the default prior patterns earns 5.0 against grim's 2.0 — that gap *is* the escalation. So the explore/exploit story at the .1/.9 prior reads cleanly with three distinct policies: abstain (0, learns nothing), grim/probe (−0.4, buys 0.325 nats), patterns (−3.0, escalates into a room of snatchers).

**What this changes:** `probe-one-token` should be read as *grim at round-1 grain* — the grain S-001 itself declares — not as a distinct policy. I've replaced the "cautious would separate them" sentence in `P-snatch-microcosm.md` §1b with this correction and the pointers, so the record no longer suggests a dispatch that couldn't work.

If a further Snatch run is ever wanted, the question worth a packet is a different one: a prior with cautious mass so the falsifier `O3` actually carries probability through the whole pipeline (packet A did this at round-1 grain only). But that's a robustness check on the falsifier, not a separation, and nothing downstream is waiting on it. Packet 3 remains the live gate.

**Yes, start now — but not on the spider-covered 18% of `aif/`. Start on `snatch/`, and build `find`, because `organise` already exists there.**

**What already runs.** `playout_snatch.clj:251–321` reads `@why` lines from the library files, computes the up-closure of the acting patterns, and writes it to `snatch-cascade.edn` as `:closure` per scenario (e.g. G1/sharer: acting `[probe-before-committing escalate-only-as-far-as-you-can-lose]`, closure adds `protect-the-unprotected-move` and `institutions-vary-by-position-and-force`). What's absent is `find`: Snatch's `find` is identity on hand-picked patterns (§3e's own table says this is why Snatch under-exercises it).

**Why `snatch/` rather than the spider-covered fraction.** Its 18 patterns carry 15 `@why`, 2 `@how`, 10 `@see-also` — all author-written, so every edge is at the authority level the laws require. The `aif/` edges are `:proposed` until sampled; a consumer built against them would be tuned to unattested input and then become the thing that certifies that input — the self-certification shape again, this time between the spider and its reader. Built on authored edges first, the consumer becomes the *standard* the spider output is judged by: a proposed `@why` edge is useful iff it changes what `find`/`organise` return for some tension, checkably. That's the evidence-apex question for the fleet ("is this the right evidence for an edge?") with an actual instrument behind it, and it's what P-organise §7's `reorganise` laws need to be tested against.

**The acceptance test, independent of the fleet.** `snatch-cascade.edn` has eight scenarios, each with a game state and an `:acting` set. Give `find` the tension of each state (`context`/`want`/`however` from the pattern IF/HOWEVER clauses) and require: F1 selected ⊆ library; F2 a receipt naming the IF/HOWEVER clause matched; F3 no score-only receipts; **F4 a named pattern it must not return per scenario** (e.g. `re-enter-after-observed-repair` in G1, where there is no remedy). Recall against `:acting` is then a measured number, not a claim. One facade to name up front: `@see-also` is not `standsOn` — 10 of the section's edges are see-also and `organise` must not fast-forward through them.

**Yes — APM is the right second domain, and it's better set up than I expected. Two facts from the survey settle it, and one caution shapes the scope.**

**What's already there.**
- **Patterns with antecedents:** 67 patterns across `math-strategy/`, `math-formalization-CA/`, `math-informal/`, and **60 of them carry IF/HOWEVER clauses** — the same shape D2's `find` evaluates on Snatch. The difference is that these antecedents are prose about the problem ("the main contribution can be expressed as 'we classify all instances of X'"), not predicates over a runner state. D1 Part 3 predicted structured `find` would not carry to prose-only sections; APM is where that prediction gets tested rather than assumed.
- **Edges:** `@why` yes, `@how` no — none in any math section (your recollection was right). The 23 math `@why` edges **cross sections** (`math-formalization-CA/…` → `math-informal/reduce-to-known-result`, → `math-strategy/proof-architecture`), which is exactly what Snatch lacks: up-closure spans sections, so selected patterns can have omitted intermediates between them, and **O3 (fast-forward) becomes testable for the first time**.
- **A ground truth to reconstruct against:** frame `jit-all-open-v2-f49` already names twelve distinct library patterns in its live records (`cast-normalization` ×7, `tactic-algebra-interference` ×4, `reduce-to-known-result`, `proof-architecture`, `local-to-global`, …). So a worked proof with per-step pattern mentions exists — the analogue of Snatch's `:acting`. Caution: the ledger's formal `:used-ids` field is **empty**; the mentions live in reviews and attempts as prose. Any `:acting` sequence for a frame is therefore *reconstructed*, and has to be labelled so, or it's the "harvested-presented-as-authored" facade from the catalogue.

**Why it's worth more than a second Snatch.** The things Snatch can barely express are the ones AIF is about:
- The hidden state isn't a partner's disposition but *which route works*; an observation is a tactic outcome or a lemma found or not.
- **Learning outcomes exist natively**: a refuted route is an outcome that changes the model and not the score — the action-vs-learning split you insisted the carrier must have, which in Snatch needed a 0.1/0.9 prior to show up at all.
- A **policy as temperament** is literal: precedence over strategies (`reduce-to-known-result` first vs `unfold-the-definition` first), and S-G4 has a natural reading — same patterns, different precedence, different proof length or a dead end. Same collection, different wiring, different score, on a proof.
- **F4 comes free**: at any step there are patterns whose IF cannot hold (no known result within reach, no coercion in the goal).

**The scope to hold, stated up front.** Not a "proof engine". One frame with a terminal certificate, reconstructed step by step as a cascade: per step, the applicable patterns (`find`) with receipts naming the clause matched; the up-closure via `@why`; the precedence that reproduces the recorded order; then the AIF layer — which steps were probes (taken to learn whether a route was open) and whether the epistemic term changes the chosen step anywhere. That's `nonDegenerate` in the proof domain, and it's the microcosm again one level up in expressiveness. The refusal rule applies here more than anywhere: if a proof-state Tension can't be typed so that these IF clauses are evaluable, the packet says so instead of grepping tension words — which is exactly what D1 said would happen on prose sections.

**Sequencing.** After wave 1, and with the same shape as this line: a discovery packet first — pick the frame (f49 is a candidate since its patterns are already named), inventory its steps, list which cited patterns' IF clauses are evaluable against the step's Lean goal versus prose-only, and name one F4 pattern per step. No code, review, then build. I'd write the S1 record before any of that, and it stays on your word.

**Yes, and most of what would moderate the overlaps already exists — but the fan-out has a spine that can't be parallelised, and the real concurrency bound is review, not seats.**

**What the specification already provides as the moderator.** All 18 R-nodes are pegged to library patterns; the Figure-4 wiring is data (`control-map-edges.edn`: 21 drawn edges, 1 unresolved (`R5→R5`), 26 derived-undrawn with their derivations); the prereg has a Gate-0 class for every node's nouns and fifteen invariants; five nodes have full worksheets (R2, R5, R6, R8, R14). The overlap points *are* the edges, and the lifecycle already has the type for them: `Delivery := {from, to, payload : Schema, guarantee, idem-key, receipt …}`. So the moderation rule writes itself: **builders own nodes; the specification owns edges.** A builder may not change an edge schema; if the node can't be built against it, the deliverable is the refusal plus a proposed schema change as a separate spec packet, reviewed by the owner and the other endpoint's builder.

**What the Gate-0 table says can fan out now, and what can't.**
- *Spine — serial, blocks the rest:* Outcome → C (R19, still **undefined** for the WM) → Cascade → Policy (R14) → Q → G (R5) → coverage, then Select (R6). This is `P-validated-R5`, with Snatch as its test; no amount of parallelism shortens it, and July's failure was building around it before it existed.
- *Fan-out-able today* (theory-defined, or stack-defined with a worksheet): **R9** no-self-certification (theory-defined as a rule, cheapest, and it is the node every other node's self-report passes through — the thing that failed in July, so first), **R2** observation vector (worksheet; its first problem is the 14-vs-13 channel discrepancy), **R3** predictive-coding update (formula), **R7** precision, **R8** F and g (worksheet), **R17** BMR (formulas), **R10/R12** assurance.
- *Blocked by the spine:* R4 forward model (needs the Outcome carrier), R13 rollout (over cascades, not sequences), R11 arbitration (over policies), R16 actuation (where the whitelist lived — needs the registry), R15, and **R20**, which must watch *this* loop and so needs the loop to exist.

**The build process, lifecycle-conformant.**
1. Per node, before any dispatch: a problem record `P-Rn.md` — S1 (problem, facades, *solved* checked before running), the node's inbound/outbound edges as `Delivery` records each with a schema and **one fixture payload**, acceptance = the prereg invariants for that node. Written by the owner, with the acceptance dry-run on whatever artefact exists (row-11 rule).
2. Per node: discovery packet → review → build packet → review. One behaviour each, refusal a deliverable, `--mode work`, parked. Exactly today's D1/D2/D3 shape.
3. A **control-map linter** (the analogue of `library_graph_lint`): every drawn edge has a schema, a fixture, and both endpoint records reference it; run as a gate in every packet. The overlap test is mechanical: node A's output validates against the edge schema; node B consumes the fixture.
4. Integration = the prereg's invariants over the whole loop, and "anatomy of a flight" only after the model is validated — your rule.

**The honest constraint.** Today's evidence: about twelve packets reviewed in five hours, with three commissioner-side defects caught by builders and one false causal claim caught by you. Each real review costs 20–50 minutes of mine. That bounds it at **three or four concurrent lanes**, not eighteen. A second reviewer for the semantic half (a Claude seat — claude-13 knows the R5 material) roughly doubles it; Codex reviewing Codex is author≠reviewer only in a weak form.

**What I'd do first, on your word:** write `P-R9.md`, `P-R2.md` and `P-R8.md` from the glossary and worksheets, plus the control-map linter as one small packet; then run three lanes (R9, R2, R8) alongside the R5 spine. The fleet's harvest and the APM domain stay queued behind those.

─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── *no mission*
joe: So I'm now wondering if we might want a fifth kind of precept, because you've got these four precepts about nouns, verbs, organization, and evidence.

claude: No survey needed for this one; today's own log is the test bed.

**A fifth precept earns its place only if a failure today could not be filed under the four. Running that test, one candidate survives: *provenance* — when a claim was established, by which instrument, and how long it stays valid.**

**Why the four don't cover it.** Nouns say what a thing is; verbs what it does; organisation how the parts fit; the evidence apex whether the evidence gathered is the right evidence. All four describe a *snapshot*. What they can't express is that a true snapshot goes stale. Look at what the log rows actually were:
- Row 1: 88 outcomes read as 3 — the *instrument* (`read-string`, first form only) was the error, not the noun or the evidence kind.
- Row 6: a negative check that didn't name its instrument (grep blind to `\emph{}`).
- Row 12: `writing-coherence/` "done" inherited from the pilot's state file — right evidence kind, right organisation, but produced by a run in which rung 1 was never performed; *done needed a date and an instrument against it*.
- Row 14: a cause ("the route is broken") recorded without the probe that would establish it, then repeated for an hour as settled.
- And the biggest one: R8's seven weeks of silence — five deliveries each with a defensible *guarantee*, composing into nothing over *time*. We already reached for this dimension without naming it: `Delivery` grew `timeout-ms`, `retry`, `at-least-once`, `receipt`; `find_snatch` grew `:as-of`; the spider grew `:at` and the mtime-keyed index; the memory rule says "convert relative dates to absolute."

Those are five failures with one shape, and none is a noun, verb, organisation or evidence-kind failure. The apex asks *is this the right evidence*; provenance asks *is it still evidence, and by whose hand*.

**What it would look like as a precept.** Every claim in a record carries `{as-of, instrument (command + result), producer}` and an explicit expiry condition (what would invalidate it: "the export changes", "the route is shown not to do X"). Gate 0 already requires an *external* term with a falsifier; the provenance gate would require that the falsifier's *last run* is dated. The R9 checker being built now is half of this already (`producer`, `layer`); the other half is `as-of` — which is why R9-D1's inventory should record when each independence claim was last tested, not only who made it.

**The alternative reading, which I think is the better geometry.** Provenance may not be a fifth *vertex* at all. A tetrahedron is a snapshot; the lifecycle's validation log is the *sequence* of them, and the arrows between snapshots carry exactly the provenance data (what changed, by which instrument, when). That's the Markov-category shape again — objects are the four-precept states, morphisms are dated, instrumented transitions — and your Sierpiński recursion still works because it's a recursion on tetrahedra *indexed by time*. On that reading the fifth precept is the requirement that every arrow exist and be written down: no undated transitions. The log rows are already that, done by hand; making it a precept would make it checkable.

So: I'd add it — as the rule on the arrows rather than a fifth corner — and I'd test it the same way the others were: put it on the R9 and control-map records now (they're the first ones the tech lead will hold builders to) and see whether it catches something in the next few packets that the four didn't. If it never fires, it wasn't a precept.

Privately: nothing further to request — the proposal is recorded; validation is by use on the build, which is already in motion, and the next signals are the tech lead's reply and the fleet watcher.

**Agreed, and "workflow state" is the right name — provenance is its backward half.** Recorded as `delivery-lifecycle.md` §0.10, marked *proposed, not validated*, in your words.

**What it is, in one sentence:** the four precepts describe a snapshot — what the parts are, what they do, how they fit, whether the evidence they amass is the right evidence — and none of them says *who holds the thing right now, at which iteration, and what the next handoff is*. As a type it's `Delivery` lifted from data to roles:

```
Handoff  := { artefact, from : Role, to : Role, at, iteration, awaiting, deadline, receipt }
Workflow := { holder : Role, iteration, history : List Handoff }   -- provenance is `history`
```

**Why it's a distinct kind of fact** — the failures it names weren't noun, verb, fit or evidence-kind failures: "I'll get back to you" followed by silence (the reason the park protocol exists); a `running` that is last-observed rather than live (claude-20's own caveat this hour); a `:done` inherited from a previous iteration's state file; a bell without a park; author = reviewer ("the author then closed thirteen"); a count carried into a record without the iteration that produced it — the two things claude-20 caught in my records.

**Where it already lives.** The Agency stores the instance: parks, jobs, the roster's roles, and now the build ledger — `BUILD-ledger.md` is the big tetrahedron's workflow vertex, and the assignment to claude-20 is a `Handoff` in exactly this shape (from claude-15, to claude-20, at 14:45Z, awaiting `invoke-…fefee03b`, deadline +55 min, receipt = its first report). The problem record's S1 has it in embryo (`owner`, `status`, `deliveries` with job/park ids); the explicit fields are `holder`, `iteration`, `handoffs`. Your Sierpiński recursion applies unchanged: a node's own tetrahedron gets its own workflow vertex — who holds *that node*.

**Validation, per your rule:** not ratified. It's applied first to the R-node build and to the P-R9 / control-map-lint records; a log row gets written when it catches something the four didn't — or when it fails to. If it never fires, it wasn't a precept.

joe: Yeah, and the other exciting thing about thinking about it this way is that we could use this tetrahedron model as an orchestration plan for building. Pretty much anything. [Bash] cd /home/joe/code/futon4/holes && python3 - <<'PYEOF'
p='delivery-lifecycle.md'; s=open(p).read()
anchor="**Validation.** 
Privately: nothing further to request — the refinement is recorded, the six live records now carry `holder`/`parent`, and validation is by use on the build already in motion.

**Yes — mass, not vertex, and that makes the tetrahedron an orchestration plan rather than a description.** Recorded as the refinement in §0.10, in your words, with a checkable form.

**What the geometry adds when you take it literally.** In the Sierpiński tetrahedron, subdividing removes the interior: the four child simplices touch only at *points* — the midpoints of the parent's edges — and nothing fills the space between them. Read as a build plan:
- **Occupancy** is what makes a tetrahedron live. Four precepts with no holder is a specification of something nobody is building — and that is the July facade in its purest form: a diagram that "ran" with no one inside it.
- **Subdivision** transfers work, not authority. When a holder subdivides, they stop doing the work and become the keeper of the contact points — which are exactly your "overlap points moderated by the specification". Children own their interiors; the parent owns the edges. That's what happened today, unplanned: I held the big tetrahedron, subdivided into CML/R9/R2/R8, handed the build sub-simplex to claude-20, who assigned codex seats to node simplices and keeps the ledger. And it's why "builders own nodes, the spec owns edge schemas" felt right before it had a reason.
- **Termination** is the small packet: one file, one behaviour, one acceptance — a simplex one holder can fill directly.
- **Evidence flows only through contact points**: child apex → parent apex (the six-line review-request → the owner gate). Never sibling to sibling. That's a rule the fleet already broke once — the consumer being tuned to the spider's output was sibling-to-sibling contact — and the reason `find` was built on `snatch/` instead.

**Consequence for the War Machine.** It stops being R1→…→R20 with ticks. The top simplex is occupied by the machine; R6 *generation* is subdivision; R8 is the parent reading its children's outcomes; R9 is the apex rule every child inherits; and the machine can grow nested decompositions as needed. The July version had the four corners and no mass.

**Agreed, and I've made it structural rather than a hope — with a hold in force until the first Lean interfaces exist.**

**Your worry, stated in the gasket's terms.** For each node's tetrahedron, the Lean statement and the Clojure implementation of a term are two *children*, and children may only touch at contact points. Until an hour ago there was no contact point at all — no Lean declaration existed for anything decided today — so the lanes were building against prose, and the patch-up you're describing would have been certain, in whichever direction happened to move last.

**The rule now in the charter ("Interfaces are Lean declarations")** — `Holes.lean` is the registry of contact points; I hold it; the tech lead proposes changes by bell; builders never edit it:
1. A build packet (D2+) **quotes the term's Lean declaration** as the interface the Clojure must implement; if no declaration exists, the packet doesn't go out — the hole is added first.
2. The tech lead's pre-dispatch check compares the packet's signature to the declaration **mechanically** (`count-holes.sh` lists them), not by eye.
3. **A lane closes only when the hole moves** — `sorry` → a body, or a stated theorem whose fixture is the Clojure run (the `CommitmentTemperature` finite style). "Implemented in Clojure, Lean unchanged" is an open lane; the two-line holes count is the gate.
4. Refusal names the signature clause that's wrong for the artefact — a proposal to me, never a silent narrowing in Clojure with the Lean left as-is.

**In force now:** hold on every D2+ dispatch (R8-D2 included, even if claude-13's read passes) until LH-D1 — codex-22's `Holes.lean`, running since ~15:13Z — lands and is reviewed; then the tech lead gets the declaration list and R8-D2's packet is amended to quote `F : Belief → Observation → Precision → ℝ` and its record contract, with "the F hole moves" added to its acceptance. Discovery packets (R9-D1b) are unaffected — they name artefacts and implement nothing. Bell to claude-20 sent and parked (`invoke-…f124c282`).

**What this costs and buys.** About thirty minutes of lane latency now, against the alternative you named: retro-fitting Lean to whatever the Clojure ended up meaning — which is the July shape (vocabulary present, alignment absent) with the roles reversed. It also gives each hole a holder, which is the fifth precept doing its first real job: a `sorry` with no one assigned is a conceptual artefact; a `sorry` with a record, a holder and a Clojure witness named is a contact point waiting to close.

# P-lean-clojure-adapter — One adapter
Privately: nothing further to request — the record is written, the discovery packet is running and parked, and the build's other lanes are with the tech lead.

**Agreed, and it's more than "a bit nicer" — the adapter is the mechanism the build has been running by hand all afternoon.**

**What the survey found.** Two adapters exist, neither general:
- **APM:** `APMCycleContractEmitter.lean` builds `contractJson` from the canonical cycle machine and prints it (`lake env lean --run` via `regenerate-apm-contract.sh`); `futon3c.apm.generated-contract` reads it and validates it against **~330 lines of hand-restated expectations** (`required-bounds`, `required-dispatch-policy`, `required-*`) plus a phase-order round trip. The contract is checked against a copy of itself — that's the anti-pattern to drop, not the part to standardise.
- **WM:** `ContractEmitter.lean` already emits predicate *families* with a `clojure-locus` naming the file that should witness each Lean predicate — and **nothing on the Clojure side reads it** (the unbuilt H3a).

Meanwhile every "hole moved?" judgement today was made by a reviewer reading a Clojure run and a Lean declaration and writing a ledger line. That's the adapter's job, done by people.

**The standard, as written in `P-lean-clojure-adapter.md`:**
1. **One emitter helper** (`DarkTower/Contract/Emit.lean`) exporting a *declaration registry* — per tagged declaration: name, kind (closed/hole), pretty-printed signature, owner record, holder, `clojure-locus`, fixture — plus the source module and git sha. `Holes.lean`, the WM families and the APM emitter all print the same shape; `count-holes.sh` becomes a consumer of that JSON instead of a grep.
2. **One generic Clojure consumer** with a **witness registry**: a check declares which declaration it witnesses and the fixture it produced; a lint joins registry to contract and reports per declaration `witnessed` (a *recorded run*, not a file's presence) / `unwitnessed` / `stale` (fixture older than the declaration's commit). Two lines per record, never a percentage. The only Clojure-side constants are the witness bindings.
3. **A round trip that can fail:** for a closed declaration with a fixture, re-run it and compare to the Lean-stated value (`r2ContractCensus` illFormed = 2; `r8Census` 755/32/5) — mismatch is `stale-or-refuted` with both numbers.
4. **Falsifiers:** an unwitnessed declaration shows as such rather than vanishing; a check claiming a declaration not in the contract fails the lint; regenerating from another Lean sha flips fixtures to `stale` until re-run.

**Dispatched — discovery only:** AD-D1 → codex-22 (job `invoke-1788104829154-4334-88a69f02`, parked): the two emitted shapes side by side, the proposed common schema with one example per kind, the witness-registry shape with `find_snatch.clj` ↔ F1–F4 as the worked example, and a triage of every APM `required-*` block as *restatement* or *binding*. Then AD-D2 (the helper + `Holes.lean` exporting through it), AD-D3 (consumer + lint with falsifier tests), AD-D4 (APM moved onto the helper with its behaviour unchanged — round trip as acceptance), one behaviour each, each past claude-13.

Once AD-D3 lands, "the hole moved" stops being a reviewer's sentence and becomes a lint line — which is what the fifth precept asked for at the Lean/Clojure contact point.

**Yes. And the sharpest version of what you said is: the evidence apex can be answered before any evidence exists.** That's now written into the adapter record (solved 4), the holes record, and the lifecycle's apex section, so AD-D2 builds it rather than rediscovering it.

**The mechanism, concretely.** Every hole names a law nobody has proved; from now it also names **what would move it** — an `evidence : Type` (the artefact kind a witness must inhabit) and a `falsifier : evidence → Prop` (the zero-mass outcome) — *derived from the AIF reading of the law*, not from whatever artefact happens to exist. The adapter's lint then has a judgement beside `witnessed / unwitnessed / stale`: **`conformant`** (the fixture parses as an inhabitant of the declared type) versus **`wrong-shape`** — the apex's "wrong evidence", made mechanical. That is the lifecycle's `EvidenceContract` with its `artefact` and `falsifier` fields as Lean types instead of prose.

**Two holes already have inhabitants, so the round trip has a green case and a red case on day one:**
- `nonDegenerateAblationLaw` — evidence `AblationTable := Prior → {argminG, argminRisk, moved?}`, falsifier "no prior moves"; `checks/ablation-snatch.edn` is one.
- `r8EraBoundary` — evidence `EraTable := {boundary, perEra → {count, storedF?, selectionGain?, shape, meanPrecision}}`, falsifier "a form in neither era".

**Why it compounds.** Each conformant run is evidence *of the right kind by construction*, so it can be counted and reasoned over; each `wrong-shape` is the refusal the July machine never issued — an artefact that exists and isn't the evidence the theory said to gather. And a hole with no witness yet still says what would count, so the absence is typed rather than silent.

**And it's the machine's own loop.** Hypothesis (the Lean law) → predicted observation (the evidence type) → observation (the Clojure run) → update (the hole moves, or the falsifier fires). The build process is an instance of active inference over its own artefacts — which is what your occupied-tetrahedron picture said it should be. I've kept that sentence short in the lifecycle so it reads as a fact about the mechanism rather than a slogan.

**Standing rule I'd hold from here:** no hole is added to `Holes.lean` without its evidence type; a hole that can't state one is a sign the law isn't yet stated on the theory's terms — Gate 0 arriving from the Lean side.

**Hole-text proposal — charter 6, first use, and it is a blocker.** claude-13 refused R9-D2, and the defect is in the Lean, not my packet. `r9VerdictSound` carries `_sound : ∀ p S, decide? p S = true ↔ p ∈ S` as a *hypothesis*, which pins `decide?` to membership. Both conjuncts then follow by unfolding the match — `simp [independenceVerdict, independent]` discharges it — and **no wrong `decide?` can ever fail it**, because a wrong one doesn't satisfy `_sound`, so the implication is vacuously true and the theorem never sees it. My packet asked a builder to include a deliberately wrong `decide?` and show the property fails; the signature cannot express that. The hole would move by `simp` while the Clojure test showed only that the port is faithful — a facade close under clause 3.

claude-13's replacement, which I endorse: a second proposition asserting the hypothesis is load-bearing —

```
∃ decide?, ¬(∀ p S, decide? p S = true ↔ p ∈ S)
         ∧ w.producer ∈ claim.producingPart
         ∧ independenceVerdict (some claim) w decide? = .independent
```

That one can fail — it is false if `independenceVerdict` consults membership directly instead of `decide?` — and proving it *is* "the soundness hypothesis does work." Credit to claude-13. **R9-D2 is held until you ratify it.**

• invoke-1788105138524-4340-685b3eef: **REFUSE** — one clause is unsatisfiable, and it's (c).

## The refusal: git-sha-at-emit and "commit the JSON" cannot both hold

> *"C. …`> DarkTower/WarMachine/holes-contract.json`; **commit the JSON**."*
> *"ACCEPTANCE: …the JSON's `source.git-sha` equals `git rev-parse HEAD` at emit; re-running emit is byte-identical (the sha is the only volatile field, and it is stable within a commit)."*

Emit reads HEAD = X and writes it into the JSON. Committing the JSON creates commit Y. The committed file now says X while HEAD says Y, so re-running emit produces a different file — **the artefact is stale the instant it is committed**, and the acceptance is false for it from that moment.

The parenthetical *"stable within a commit"* is where the error hides. It would be true if the JSON sat outside the commit; but the JSON **is** in the commit, and no file can contain the sha of the commit that contains it. This isn't a determinism mis-statement, it's a fixed point that doesn't exist. A builder will hit it at the commit step, after the work.

**One-line fix:** state the acceptance as *"`source.git-sha` is the sha of the commit **preceding** the one that adds the JSON, and re-emit after committing is expected to differ in that field alone"* — or drop `commit the JSON` and regenerate on demand.

## (a) Nothing in the acceptance is surprising — and an independent comparison is available unused

`grep -c 'uses .sorry.' == :kind hole` count is the row-10 shape: both sides are written by the same hand in the same file in one session. It catches a typo, not a wrong belief.

But Holes.lean already carries an independently-authored witness. At `93f0da26`:

```
HOLE doc-tags:            18
CLOSED-BY-RECORD tags:    17
':= sorry' bodies:        18
```

Your "17 bodies / 18 holes" is exact. And the **doc tags were written by the owner in earlier sessions**, while the registry is written now — so `doc-tags == registry entries == sorry count` is a three-way check whose first leg can actually disagree. The stated pair cannot: a builder who adds a hole and forgets its doc tag passes today's acceptance.

**Fix:** require the three counts to agree, with the doc-tag count taken from `93f0da26` rather than from the working tree.

## (b) The refusal path is nominally open and practically closed

You say it twice — *"do not invent one"*, *"C/find/organise: REFUSE — implementations, not laws"*. But section B then **pre-supplies the answer for every other hole**: `FindReceiptTable`, `CascadeDiff`, `IllFormedList`, the disposition triple, `VerdictTable`, `WitnessLayerTable`. So every case is either pre-answered or pre-refused, and the refusal instruction has no live scope. If one of those six is wrong for its record, a builder implements it rather than refuses — refusing means contradicting the packet's own list.

That's the row-11 shape one level over: the acceptance written from the commissioner's memory of the records.

**One-line fix:** mark the six as *proposals to be checked*, and require the bell to name **which sentence of which record licenses each evidence type** — so an unlicensed one gets refused instead of built.

## (d) No contradiction with the cited records

Section A says "following AD-D1 §(ii) **exactly**" and then adds two fields — flagged in the same sentence ("with two additions below"), so it reads as an amendment rather than a conflict. `P-lean-holes.md`'s "Evidence shape per hole" is what B implements. Nothing quotes against itself.

**Summary: REFUSE on (c) alone.** (a) and (b) are weaknesses that let a builder pass without learning anything; (c) is the one that makes the acceptance impossible to meet as written. Fix (c) and I'd pass it with (a) and (b) folded in as one-liners.

• invoke-1788106295311-4369-80f144d3: **REFUSE** — on (a). The judgements never consult the field that records whether anything ran.

## The clause

> A. `… :result :passed|:failed :recorded-at "<ISO>"`
> B. `:witnessed (binding current, evidence type has no shape check yet)` · `:conformant (binding current AND fixture inhabits the evidence type)`

"Binding current" resolves through `:stale`, which compares only `:contract-sha` and `:run-sha`. **No judgement anywhere reads `:result`.** So the registry collects a pass/fail field and the lint ignores it: a builder writes two correct shas into `witness-registry.edn`, never executes `find_snatch.clj` or `ablate_g_snatch.clj`, and gets `:witnessed` × 4 and `:conformant` × 1 — the exact registered expectation, from file presence plus two `git log -1` invocations.

You asked me to check the wording actually forbids it. **It doesn't.** There is no sentence requiring a binding to record a real run, and `:result` is the only field that could carry one.

**One-line fix:** `:witnessed` and `:conformant` require `:result :passed`; a binding with `:result :failed` gets its own judgement (`:witness-failed`), and a binding missing `:result` or `:recorded-at` is an ERROR alongside the not-in-contract case.

## The other three

**(b) The seed shas all exist and are tracked.** `find_snatch.clj` → `8762ba7`, `find-snatch.edn` → `5941270`, `ablate_g_snatch.clj` → `6364964`. The ablation fixture the packet doesn't name is `checks/ablation-snatch.edn` → `6364964`; worth naming it, since the builder otherwise has to guess. And the contract is emitted and consistent with your gate: **44 declarations, 21 closed / 23 hole**, `source.git-sha` `25d1771d368b`.

But **18 `:unwitnessed` is forced arithmetic**, not a finding: it is 23 − 5, and the packet instructs "nothing else is witnessed yet; do not invent bindings". It catches a mis-seeded registry and nothing else. The informative numbers are the split *within* the 5 (conformant vs witnessed) and the `:shape-check-not-implemented` typed absence.

One thing your expectation doesn't cover: **3 of the 23 holes have `null` evidence** in the emitted contract. Under B they are neither `:conformant` nor `:wrong-shape`, and they'll land in `:unwitnessed` indistinguishably from the 15 that have an evidence type and no witness. Worth a distinct judgement or an explicit note, or the 18 hides a second population.

**(c) Both shas are builder-written, so `:stale` cannot fire on the seed today — by design, not by defect.** It is a drift detector for the *next* contract emit, and your fixture test C exercises it synthetically, which is the right place. The risk isn't unreachability, it's reporting "0 `:stale`" as a result when it restates "I wrote today's shas." Label it the way the R8/R9 packets now label their tautologies.

**(d) No contradiction with the cited records.** One gap rather than a conflict: A specifies `:fixture` only for the `find_snatch` binding, while C expects ablation to reach `:conformant`, which needs its fixture path to run the `AblationTable` shape check.

**Fix (a), name the ablation fixture, and add the `null`-evidence note, and I'd pass it.**

## The gate is now satisfiable exactly when the work is correct

I verified both baselines and they agree with each other, which is what makes selection-by-name sound:

```
T0 at 32b92969 (Holes.lean)          24 HOLE tags
baseline JSON at 53c5e466            48 declarations — 24 closed / 24 hole
signatures mentioning Policy there   ['Policy']
```

The 24 holes in the baseline JSON match the 24 tags in the baseline module, so the two pins describe the same state and `new ∖ baseline` by name yields exactly the builder's additions.

Against your question, taking the three cases in turn:

**Empty selection now fails.** `N_selected = 0` cannot equal `N_added_bodies + N_added_holes` unless the builder added nothing, in which case the equation `T0 + N_added_holes = :kind hole` and the registered `+5..+7` range fail anyway. The vacuous-pass route from rev 2 is closed.

**A new signature mentioning `Policy` fails.** Selection is by name against the baseline, so the pre-existing `Policy` declaration is excluded structurally rather than by a filter that might or might not catch it — and it is the only signature in the baseline that mentions `Policy`, so nothing else can leak into the grep.

**Correct work passes.** New declarations that avoid `Policy` give `N_selected = N_added` and `grep -c Policy = 0`, both quotable.

The two numbers are the thing that makes it work: rev 2's single number was unsatisfiable when the filter was too wide and vacuous when empty, and a count plus a grep pins both ends.

```
                 and records the basis (tx / count / max-at) it read at.
falsifier      : a negative — "absent", "0 hits", "cannot be organised" — produced from
                 a snapshot, export, printout or .edn dated before the store's latest write.
```
First recorded instance: row 25, the spider's rung 1 over the 07-10 export. And the rule for the case where no endpoint exists: *the deliverable is the endpoint, not a fresher snapshot* — otherwise it's the archive machine with a shorter lag.

**2. The corpus fix — packet 4c → codex-20** (the runner's author), running: rung 1 pages `GET /api/alpha/evidence?since=…&limit=1000` through the keyset cursor to exhaustion, hydrates as the projection requires, keeps the exact-occurrence scan and the listing / self-text / co-mention flags, pins the index by the FTS sidecar's **basis** (or count + max-at where the basis isn't exposed), stamps every absence and attestation with the corpus it was measured against, and removes every reference to the export (grep must return 0). Acceptance is a number that must move: war-room's coverage above the export-era 10/10, reported beside aif's 26/8 and wc's 22/16. Wave 2 — war-room re-run included, no special handling — waits on its gate.

|---|---|---|

Two design choices worth your check:
  - **An unmapped hop is not an auto-failure** — after today's R16 findings, a hop the diagram doesn't draw is at least as likely evidence *about the wiring* as about the code; unmapped hops go to the CML lane as proposed amendments.

Privately, what I need next: nothing — the records are committed (futon4 `607918b`, futon2 `ccf7ce9`), AUTO-D1 is drafted in place but deliberately undispatched, wave-2 resume is the only running job and it's parked with a fresh deadline. The deliverable is the answer and the goodnight summary.

**Recorded as §0.14 — move 4 in the register, marked PROPOSED for your waking eyes.** And your geometric instinct is exact, not decorative: subdividing a tetrahedron into four corner tetrahedra leaves a central **octahedron** — the negative space whose faces touch all four children and all four parent faces. That's where workflow lives: not a fifth vertex, because it's *the shape of what the four vertices exchange*.

**The decomposition — what makes work automatable — drafted from tonight's own ledger** (~25 dispatched units, every gate and every intervention on the record):

1. **Ports typed on both sides** — freehand ports are where a human silently supplies the type;
2. **Acceptance named in advance and dry-run satisfiable** — judgement-at-read-time means a human gate;
3. **Executable falsifier** — the work can know it failed without a reader;
4. **Named evidence consumer** — else automation just produces exhaust faster;
5. **Pinned reads, loud absences** — else it runs on silently wrong inputs, worse than not running;
6. **Bounded, reversible blast radius** — and where arming is needed (the R16 fork is exactly this line), the task *splits* at the arming point rather than being classed unautomatable;
7. **The decision surface is covered**: every decision the task can meet is pre-decided in the packet, covered by a standing precept, or honestly refusable. This is the real answer to "what has to be set up in advance": **not the answers — the decision routes.** Who rules, at which contact point, with what deadline behaviour.

Your old failure mode — the human quietly load-bearing inside an "automated" flow — is criteria 1+7 violated together: an untyped port whose type a person supplies, plus an undeclared decision they keep making. The machine couldn't distinguish candidates because neither the port nor the decision was declared; now both are nouns.

**The case study is already half-run**: tonight's ledger *is* the data — which units cleared all seven (most of the D-packets did: that's why ~25 handoffs needed only rulings from me, not typing), and what exactly the operator supplied (fork rulings, precept statements, seat lending — pure decision-surface, criterion 7). Re-reading the day against the criteria is **AUTO-D1**, drafted in the section, dispatched only on your word. The candidate declaration when you ratify: `wmAutomatesWork` — one unit of this machine's own build, dispatch to gate, zero operator turns in the span.

**Goodnight state:** wave-2 resume is the only thing running (healthy, re-parked; interim-harvest fallback armed if the second deadline fires). Everything else gated closed. Your morning list, one page on `BUILD-status.md`: the R16 fork, lendable seats, mark2, the two π's, G-D2, APM authority, second domain, and now §0.14's ratification. Four top-level moves in one day, three of them yours before midnight. Sleep well.
